## [FedPA-LoRA tackles aggregation and initialization mismatch in heterogeneous federated LoRA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15381) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces FedPA-LoRA, a product-aligned federated LoRA framework that keeps local factors across rounds to eliminate initialization mismatch and aggregates heterogeneous-rank updates in product space to reduce aggregation mismatch, with convergence bounds for both homogeneous and heterogeneous ranks. Experiments on NLU and generation tasks report a 6.82 percentage-point gain in average GLUE accuracy under heterogeneous ranks, and code is released on GitHub. Federated LoRA fine-tuning is increasingly used to adapt large language models without sharing data, but factor-wise aggregation and product-space reconstruction each introduce different errors that hurt convergence. FedPA-LoRA addresses both mismatches simultaneously and supports client-specific ranks, which matters for real deployments where devices have different compute and communication budgets. The server aggregates heterogeneous-rank updates in product space and reconstructs a rank-constrained global adapter without forming the dense aggregate, while decoupling local and reference ranks lets clients use different computation and communication budgets. The reported 6.82 percentage-point GLUE improvement is measured under heterogeneous ranks, and the method comes with convergence bounds for both homogeneous and heterogeneous settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 3, 05:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) fine-tunes large models by learning a low-rank product of two small matrices instead of updating all weights, which greatly reduces memory and communication costs. In federated learning, clients train locally and a server aggregates their updates, but LoRA's factorized form makes aggregation ambiguous: averaging the two factors directly can distort the product, while reconstructing the product first can break the continuity of locally optimized factors. Heterogeneous ranks—clients using different LoRA ranks—further complicate aggregation because factor shapes no longer match across clients.

The paper introduces a four-coefficient per-token gating parameterization λ_t = σ(a·h_t + b·u(x) + c + d·gap_t) for on-policy knowledge distillation, showing that existing EOPD and ToDi methods are one-dimensional restrictions of this family. Experiments on TweetEval emotion and hate tasks with a Qwen3-32B teacher and Qwen3-4B student show the full family outperforms matched single-channel restrictions in 33 of 36 comparable cells. This work provides a shared coordinate system for comparing per-token gating designs in on-policy distillation, potentially guiding future method development and improving distillation efficiency for LLM training. It affects researchers and practitioners working on knowledge distillation and LLM training. The full family adds multi-channel composition and an explicit bias as extra degrees of freedom; a 26-cell mean-match isolation experiment places dynamic gating ahead of effective-KL-matched static baselines in 19 of 26 cells. However, the authors report these as exploratory aggregate directional evidence, and three-seed paired replications of nine headline comparisons are directionally consistent but not significant at n=3.

## [Fine-Tuning Gemma-4 MoE VLMs Improves 2D and 3D Rotation Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04206) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.04206v3) fine-tunes Google DeepMind's Gemma-4 mixture-of-experts (MoE) vision-language models and generalist VLMs on object-rotation datasets, showing that fine-tuned MoE models significantly outperform fine-tuned generalist models in predicting rotations defined by axes and angles. Fine-tuning also substantially improved angle estimation for 2D representations without requiring an explicit coordinate system. Spatial reasoning is a known weakness of vision-language models, limiting their use in STEM, medicine, architecture, and construction. Demonstrating that fine-tuning, especially with MoE architectures, can improve rotation understanding suggests a practical path to enhance AI's spatial intelligence for real-world tasks. The study found that identifiable objects did not improve angle-detection accuracy; instead, objects with prominent linear features showed better performance. The abstract is truncated, and no community discussion is available to validate the significance of the findings.

<a id="item-158"></a>
## [WeFair: Fidelity-Aware Human Feedback Alignment for Personalized Query Rewriting in Short-Video Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16927) ⭐️ 6.0/10

WeChat researchers propose WeFair, a fidelity-aware alignment framework that ranks human feedback (demonstration, preference, verification) by how directly it reflects personalized intent, and trains a 2B-parameter query rewriter through a staged curriculum of supervised fine-tuning, direct preference optimization, and group relative policy optimization. WeFair-2B has been deployed in WeChat short-video search, serving over 100 million queries per day, with an online A/B test showing a 2.593% relative increase in effective video-view rate and a 2.350% relative reduction in query reformulation rate. This work addresses a key risk in personalized search: over-weighting past consumption behavior can pull rewritten queries toward engaging content and away from the user's immediate intent, a phenomenon the authors call intent drift. By introducing a principled hierarchy of feedback fidelity and validating it in a production system at WeChat scale, the paper offers a practical recipe for balancing personalization with intent preservation in latency-sensitive search applications. The framework mines real queries and user reformulations from search sessions, screens them for contextual support, and then applies a three-stage training pipeline: supervised fine-tuning on context-to-query learning and human reformulation/preservation, direct preference optimization on subsequent consumption feedback, and group relative policy optimization that rewards exact agreement with reformulation or preservation targets to constrain intent drift. Analysis shows that broad supervision benefits the full curriculum supervised training, while strict verification outperforms auxiliary rewards.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 3, 05:00

**Background**: Query rewriting is a core technique in information retrieval that reformulates ambiguous or underspecified user queries into clearer, more retrievable forms, and LLM-based rewriters have recently improved this by leveraging synthetic reasoning and behavioral feedback. Human feedback alignment is the process of fine-tuning language models to better match human preferences, typically through stages such as supervised fine-tuning and preference optimization. Short-video search poses unique challenges because queries are often short and ambiguous, and personalization must be inferred from limited context under strict latency constraints, while avoiding intent drift—the tendency for personalization signals to distort the user's actual search goal.

## [MuSID-KD: Multigranularity Knowledge Distillation for Efficient Generative Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35218) ⭐️ 6.0/10

MuSID-KD introduces a knowledge distillation framework that combines Hierarchical Candidate Distribution Alignment (HCDA) and Ranking Impact Guided Levelwise Distillation (RIGD) to transfer knowledge from a large generative recommendation teacher to lightweight students. HCDA aligns candidate distributions at successive SID prefix levels, while RIGD allocates supervision based on each student score's contribution to pairwise ranking loss. Generative recommendation with semantic IDs suffers from high inference cost due to autoregressive decoding over multiple SID positions, and this work offers a way to reduce that cost without changing the student's online inference architecture. It could make such systems more practical for latency-sensitive and resource-constrained deployment scenarios. Experiments on real-world recommendation datasets with three lightweight student architectures show improved recommendation accuracy and better alignment of prefix marginal distributions and pairwise item rankings. The method does not modify the student's online inference architecture, and code is available via an anonymous repository.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 3, 05:00

**Background**: Generative recommendation with semantic IDs represents each item as a sequence of discrete tokens (SIDs) and predicts the next item's SID autoregressively, which is powerful but requires repeated decoding steps. Knowledge distillation typically trains a small student model to mimic a large teacher, but conventional objectives like item-level distribution matching do not account for the multi-token, hierarchical nature of SID generation.

## [SpectRAG uses weighted chunk graphs and spectral relevance for token-budgeted multi-hop RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36991) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SpectRAG introduces a spectral graph-augmented retrieval framework that builds weighted chunk graphs encoding semantic similarity, document adjacency, section hierarchy, shared entities, citation links, and LLM-generated utility question similarity. At query time, it seeds candidates via exact inner-product search, expands over the graph, re-scores using graph proximity and spectral coordinates, and selects context under a fixed token budget. This work addresses key limitations of dense retrieval in multi-hop RAG—redundant passages, missed cross-chunk dependencies, and incomplete evidence—by explicitly modeling structural relationships. It could improve evidence coverage and generation quality for complex, cross-document question answering, benefiting RAG system builders and LLM application developers. Experiments on MultiHopRAG, UltraDomain, and long-document QA show improved broad evidence recovery and competitive generation quality against dense, hybrid, HyDE, LightRAG, and NaiveRAG baselines. The authors note trade-offs in early precision, computational cost, and domain-dependent performance, indicating graph-spectral structure is most useful for inference-oriented and cross-document retrieval.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 3, 05:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) enhances LLMs by retrieving external evidence to condition generation, but standard top-k dense retrieval often returns redundant chunks and misses dependencies needed for multi-hop reasoning. Multi-hop RAG requires combining evidence across multiple passages or documents, and token-budgeted context selection aims to fit the most useful evidence into a fixed LLM context window. Spectral methods use graph eigenvalues/eigenvectors to capture global structure, while relevance propagation techniques like layer-wise relevance propagation (LRP) attribute importance by propagating signals through a network.

## [Time-Aware Denoising Boosts Collaborative Filtering Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.09181) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces TintCF, a simple yet effective mechanism that generates time-aware user/item embeddings to capture both global preferences and temporal patterns. It proposes two strategies, Tint-A (reweighting adjacency matrix) and Tint-L (reweighting loss function), to leverage time information for denoising collaborative filtering, with experiments on four real-world datasets showing efficacy. Noisy interactions, especially from implicit feedback and malicious users, significantly degrade recommendation performance, and existing denoising methods overlook the temporal dimension. This work shows that time information can effectively identify and rectify noisy edges, offering a lightweight enhancement to neural graph collaborative filtering models. The mechanism generates time-aware embeddings that are used to reweight either the adjacency matrix (Tint-A) or the loss function (Tint-L), and experiments on four datasets demonstrate that time information helps identify and rectify noisy edges. The code is available on GitHub, and the approach is presented as simple yet effective, though it is an incremental improvement over existing denoising methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 3, 05:00

**Background**: Neural graph collaborative filtering (NGCF) leverages user-item interaction graphs to learn embeddings by propagating information across high-order connections, effectively injecting collaborative signals. However, interaction data, especially implicit feedback (e.g., clicks, purchases), often contains noise from accidental clicks or malicious users, which can distort embeddings and degrade recommendations. Denoising aims to purify the input graph by identifying and correcting noisy edges, and prior methods typically rely on embedding correlations without considering temporal dynamics.

## [PPS: Plug-and-Play Saccadic Vision for Fine-Grained Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15688) ⭐️ 6.0/10

PPS introduces a lightweight, plug-and-play saccadic vision extension that adds a priority head and a cross-attention fusion block to any pretrained backbone, enabling efficient fine-grained classification via glimpse-based inference. The method improves consistently over its backbones across nine benchmarks. This approach reduces compute and memory costs for fine-grained classification by decoupling peak memory from input resolution, making high-resolution analysis more accessible. It offers a bio-inspired, modular solution that can be easily integrated into existing models, potentially benefiting applications requiring detailed visual discrimination. The priority head is trained with a per-glimpse information-gain target from a lightweight per-image memory bank, avoiding reinforcement learning. Complexity scales linearly with the number of glimpses, and a sequential sampler with non-maximum suppression selects glimpses at runtime.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 3, 05:00

**Background**: Fine-grained visual classification often requires high-resolution inputs to capture subtle details, but this increases computational load. Saccadic vision is inspired by human eye movements, where rapid saccades and fixations allow efficient scanning of scenes. Glimpse-based inference processes selected high-resolution crops to focus on informative regions, and cross-attention fusion combines features from different sources.

## [DiTGaze: Geometry-Conditioned Diffusion Augmentation for Few-Shot Gaze Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.11231) ⭐️ 6.0/10

DiTGaze introduces a geometry-conditioned latent Diffusion Transformer (DiT) that replaces the convolutional rendering stage of a two-stream 3D Gaussian Splatting backbone for gaze augmentation. On ETH-XGaze, it reduces generator-level gaze error by 4.1% relative to GazeGaussian and ranks first at every calibration budget from one to nine real images. This work shows that combining 3D Gaussian Splatting with diffusion transformers can significantly improve few-shot gaze personalization, reducing the need for burdensome dense labeled data. It could benefit applications in AR/VR, human-computer interaction, and driver monitoring where per-user calibration is costly. The method uses a learnable projection and frozen VAE to bridge rasterized Gaussian features to latent tokens, integrates timestep and identity/expression conditioning via Adaptive Layer Normalization, and applies intermediate yaw-pitch sampling plus an orthogonality constraint to reduce interference between gaze, pose, and expression. At N=9 calibration images, it reduces subject-level estimator error by 0.73 degrees and improves 14 of 15 identities (p < 0.001).

**Background**: Few-shot gaze estimation aims to learn person-specific gaze models from very few calibration samples (e.g., ≤9 images), which is challenging because over-parameterized networks tend to overfit. 3D Gaussian Splatting is a real-time radiance field rendering technique that represents scenes as 3D Gaussians and rasterizes them efficiently. Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) are a class of diffusion models based on the transformer architecture, known for scalability and high-quality generation.

The paper introduces InstruBPM, a reproducible method for instruction-tuning open-weight LLMs with parameter-efficient fine-tuning and quantization to generate BPMN process models from natural language descriptions. The compact tuned model outperforms untuned open-weight baselines and strong proprietary models on sequence and structural metrics while requiring substantially fewer resources. This work lowers the barrier to business process modeling by enabling cost-effective, privacy-preserving on-premises deployment of LLMs for generating BPMN diagrams, which could increase modeling adoption among practitioners who lack expertise. It also demonstrates that small, tuned open models can rival proprietary models on domain-specific structured generation tasks. The evaluation combines text/code similarity (BLEU, ROUGE-L, METEOR), structural fidelity (Relative Graph Edit Distance), guideline conformance via external tool checks, and a small expert review. The method uses a curated subset of a multi-domain BPMN dataset and shares trained models and scripts for reproducibility.

## [Variational decomposition autoencoding improves disentanglement of latent representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.06844) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces variational decomposition autoencoding (VDA), a framework that incorporates a strong structural bias toward signal decomposition into variational representation learning. It is instantiated as variational decomposition autoencoders (DecVAEs), encoder-only networks that perform latent autoencoding at the distribution level to learn multiple latent subspaces aligned with time-frequency characteristics. DecVAEs improve disentanglement quality and robustness over VAE-based methods on simulated and three speech datasets, positioning decomposition-aware architectures as tools for extracting structured representations from speech and other dynamic signals. This could benefit clinical diagnostics, human-computer interaction, and adaptive neurotechnologies. The method is evaluated on simulated data and three public scientific datasets for speech recognition, dysarthric speech evaluation, and emotional speech classification. Latent subspaces become factor-specific only when generative factors have distinct spectral signatures, indicating a limitation for factors without such signatures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 3, 05:00

**Background**: Variational autoencoders (VAEs) are generative models that learn latent representations by encoding data into a probabilistic latent space and decoding back to the data space. Disentangled representation learning aims to separate the underlying factors of variation in data, which is challenging for nonstationary signals like speech that vary over time and frequency. Signal decomposition methods break down nonstationary signals into amplitude- and frequency-modulated components, and VDA combines this idea with variational inference.

Sparkle introduces a scalable data generation pipeline that decouples foreground and background guidance with strict quality filtering, producing a dataset of approximately 140K video pairs across five background-change themes. The work also releases Sparkle-Bench, the largest evaluation benchmark for background replacement to date, and a trained model that substantially outperforms existing baselines on both OpenVE-Bench and Sparkle-Bench. Background replacement is central to creative applications like film production and advertising, but it has been underexplored due to scarce high-quality training data, causing state-of-the-art models such as Kiwi-Edit to produce static, unnatural backgrounds. By open-sourcing the dataset, benchmark, model, and full pipeline, Sparkle lowers the barrier for research and development in instruction-guided video editing. The authors trace the poor quality of existing data to a lack of precise background guidance during synthesis, and their decoupled pipeline addresses this by generating foreground and background guidance separately with strict quality filtering. The dataset spans five common background-change themes, and all resources are fully open-sourced at the project page.

## [Study Reveals Optimization Conflict Between Image- and Text-Based Person ReID](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02242) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.02242v2) analyzes the conflicting training objectives of image-based (I2I) and text-based (T2I) person re-identification, showing empirically that a single unconstrained vision encoder cannot jointly satisfy both. The authors propose a controlled two-stage pipeline where a shared vision encoder is first trained for I2I invariance and then frozen while only the text encoder is aligned to the fixed visual space. This work highlights a fundamental obstacle to unifying image- and text-based person re-identification in a single model, which is important for building flexible surveillance and retrieval systems that can search for people using either images or natural language descriptions. The findings suggest that naive joint training may be counterproductive and point toward staged training strategies. The conflict arises because I2I ReID contracts intra-identity distances to achieve invariance across cameras, while T2I ReID requires instance-specific textual descriptions that preserve those distances. Experiments show that I2I pre-training improves subsequent T2I performance, and adding textual supervision during pre-training further helps T2I even though the vision encoder is frozen during cross-modal training.

**Background**: Person re-identification (ReID) is a computer vision task that aims to match the same individual across different camera views, typically using appearance features like clothing and body shape. Image-based ReID (I2I) queries with an image of a person, while text-based ReID (T2I) queries with a natural language description. Multimodal learning seeks to integrate different data types, such as images and text, into a single model, but combining these two ReID objectives is challenging due to their differing supervision signals.

## [DAUPNet: Uncertainty-Aware Prototype Discrimination for Cross-Domain Few-Shot Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16308) ⭐️ 6.0/10

DAUPNet introduces a unified framework that reformulates cross-domain prototype matching as uncertainty-aware prototype discrimination, harmonizing hierarchical support-query features, representing foreground and background prototypes probabilistically, and using estimated uncertainty to regulate contrastive optimization. It achieves 72.6% and 76.7% average mIoU in 1-shot and 5-shot settings on four target domains, with substantial gains on two medical domains. This work addresses the reliability of prototype matching under severe domain shift, a core challenge in cross-domain few-shot semantic segmentation, and shows that modeling prototype uncertainty improves robustness and interpretability. The notable gains on medical domains suggest potential impact for low-resource clinical imaging applications where labeled data are scarce. The framework explicitly handles inconsistent hierarchical responses that corrupt support representations and treats prototypes with different reliability unequally during optimization to strengthen foreground-background separation. Code is available at https://github.com/madness-Lei/DAUPNet, though the approach is evaluated only on four standard target domains and remains a specialized arXiv contribution without broad community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 3, 05:00

**Background**: Cross-domain few-shot semantic segmentation (CD-FSS) aims to train models that can segment novel classes in unseen target domains using only a few labeled support images, typically by learning domain-invariant representations or improving support-query correspondence. Prototype-based methods extract a representative feature (prototype) from support images and match it to query pixels, but large domain shifts make such matching unreliable because deterministic prototypes cannot express boundary and appearance ambiguity.

<a id="item-170"></a>
## [HyCO hybrid solver blends RL and diffusion for combinatorial optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07990) ⭐️ 6.0/10

HyCO proposes a hybrid inference algorithm that uses a sequential reinforcement learning (RL) solver to construct an initial solution prefix and then adaptively switches to a conditional diffusion model (DM) to complete the remaining decisions. The paper introduces a unified error-scaling theoretical framework proving that the hybrid structure achieves strictly lower expected regret than either backbone alone and that a unique optimal trigger step exists, and it designs a lightweight adaptive trigger based on policy entropy and RL-DM disagreement. This work offers a principled way to combine the complementary strengths of sequential RL solvers and global diffusion solvers, potentially improving solution quality for neural combinatorial optimization across diverse benchmarks. It is of interest to researchers working on hybrid and learning-based solvers for NP-hard optimization problems. The theoretical analysis shows that RL solvers suffer from horizon-wise compounding errors with super-linear regret growth, while diffusion solvers incur linear or sublinear regret in the remaining unsolved subspace; the optimal trigger step is defined at the expected-regret level and is not directly computable per trajectory, so the practical trigger uses policy entropy and RL-DM disagreement as a proxy. Code and checkpoints are released at https://github.com/wmd3i/HyCO.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 3, 05:00

**Background**: Neural combinatorial optimization (NCO) uses machine learning models to solve NP-hard problems such as routing and scheduling. Sequential RL solvers build solutions step by step and are strong early but accumulate errors over long horizons, whereas diffusion models generate solutions globally and avoid step-wise compounding but scale with the dimension of the remaining problem. HyCO hybridizes the two under an optimization-regret view to exploit their complementary failure modes.

<a id="item-171"></a>
## [DINOv3-based Encoder-only Mask Transformer Ensemble Wins PalmCity Challenge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06063) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A PalmCity challenge submission compared nine pretrained segmentation systems under equal compute budgets and won with an ensemble of three DINOv3-based Encoder-only Mask Transformer models, achieving 57.08% mIoU on the hidden test leaderboard in the October 5, 2026 snapshot. A second-stage ensemble of four models, trained on both training and validation images, reached 61.54% mIoU and 71.93% mean F1, ranking first among five entries in the October 30, 2026 snapshot. This work provides practical engineering insights for compute-budgeted model selection and ensembling in semantic segmentation, showing that a minimalist ViT-based architecture with strong self-supervised features can outperform more complex alternatives. It also highlights the importance of rigorous evaluation protocols, such as paired image bootstrap and pre-registered selection rules, in small-dataset challenges. The winning ensemble uses equal-probability averaging of three EoMT models with DINOv3 features, evaluated at three image scales with horizontal reflection; training at 768×1536 and 896×1792 pixels gave the largest gains. However, validation scores are optimistic because validation images guided many choices and final models were trained on them, making the test score the only independent measurement; techniques like Segment Anything voting and instance mask overlays reduced validation performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 3, 05:00

**Background**: Semantic segmentation assigns a class label to every pixel in an image, and mean Intersection over Union (mIoU) is a standard metric for evaluating its accuracy. DINOv3 is a family of self-supervised vision foundation models that produce high-quality dense features without fine-tuning, and the Encoder-only Mask Transformer (EoMT) is a minimalist segmentation architecture that repurposes a plain Vision Transformer to jointly process image patches and segmentation queries. The PalmCity challenge focuses on panoramic street segmentation, where small datasets and unequal training costs complicate model selection.

<a id="item-172"></a>
## [DDPG Agent Dynamically Orchestrates DE and CMA-ES for Structural Model Updating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11546) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes DRL-DCO, a Deep Deterministic Policy Gradient (DDPG)-based actor-critic agent that continuously governs the evolutionary process by dynamically reallocating computational resources between Differential Evolution (DE) and CMA-ES. The agent uses a progression-aware state representation and a diversity-informed reward to jointly regulate population size, elite preservation, and restart mechanisms, and can operate in a supervision-free inference mode after training. This work moves beyond static hybrid evolutionary algorithms by using reinforcement learning to adaptively coordinate exploration and exploitation, potentially improving convergence accuracy and robustness for high-dimensional, non-convex optimization problems such as structural model updating. It demonstrates a novel integration of deep reinforcement learning with evolutionary computation, which could inspire more adaptive hybrid optimizers. The method is validated on high-dimensional single-objective optimization benchmarks and the IASC-ASCE structural health monitoring benchmark, outperforming state-of-the-art adaptive and hybrid evolutionary algorithms as well as single-operator DRL-governed baselines. The trained actor can orchestrate DE and CMA-ES through forward inference alone, without critic evaluation or weight updates, enabling faster deployment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 3, 05:00

**Background**: Differential Evolution (DE) is a population-based evolutionary algorithm that explores the search space using difference vectors, while CMA-ES is an evolution strategy that adapts a covariance matrix to guide exploitation. Hybrid algorithms combining both often use fixed switching rules, leading to disjointed search phases. Deep Deterministic Policy Gradient (DDPG) is an off-policy actor-critic reinforcement learning algorithm designed for continuous action spaces, which here learns to control the evolutionary process.

## [DEX: Digit-Level Early Exit for Energy-Efficient MSDF Neural Network Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11748) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper presents an MSDF accelerator for quantized U-Net brain-tumor segmentation that uses four runtime mechanisms—exact early negative detection, exact sign-only decision making, calibrated low-order-digit skipping, and calibrated pruning—to reduce digit cycles by 38.38% while achieving a mean Dice score of 80.58% on 73 held-out BraTS cases, compared to 81.20% for the floating-point model. This work demonstrates that digit-level early exit in MSDF arithmetic can significantly reduce computation for segmentation networks without retraining or modifying stored weights, offering a promising direction for energy-efficient medical-imaging accelerators. The processing element, synthesized in 45 nm, operates at 500 MHz, occupies 0.858 mm², and consumes 0.726 mJ per 192×192 patch; a projected eight-output accelerator with shared activation delivery achieves 16.6 ms latency and 1.67 mJ per patch, while the exact mechanisms alone reduce cycles by 18.79% without altering the quantized output.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 3, 05:00

**Background**: Most-significant-digit-first (MSDF) arithmetic is a digit-serial computing paradigm that produces output digits from the most significant to the least significant, allowing early termination when remaining digits are unnecessary. U-Net is a convolutional neural network architecture widely used for biomedical image segmentation, and quantized INT8 inference reduces memory and compute requirements for deployment on hardware accelerators.

<a id="item-174"></a>
## [PACE: Pareto-Adaptive Compression for Efficient Native MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24005) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces PACE (Pareto-Adaptive Compression for Efficient Native MLLMs), the first visual token compression framework tailored for ViT-free native multimodal LLMs with Multimodal-RoPE. PACE decomposes attention into direction-aware saliency estimates along height and width and casts token budget allocation as Pareto optimization, maintaining near-lossless performance at 25%-50% visual token retention while delivering over 2x FLOPs reduction. Native MLLMs that tokenize pixel patches directly suffer from severe visual token redundancy, yet existing compression methods target ViT-based models and leave these architectures unexplored. PACE fills this gap and could significantly reduce inference costs for native multimodal models, which are emerging as a promising paradigm for multimodal understanding. The method leverages dimensional decoupling of positional encoding to decompose attention into two orthogonal spatial saliency estimates gated by a positional attention signal, yielding direction-aware importance with negligible overhead. It also jointly identifies optimal compression depths and retention ratios through Pareto optimization over performance preservation, inference efficiency, and spatial coverage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 3, 05:00

**Background**: Native multimodal LLMs (native MLLMs) jointly encode visual and linguistic sequences within a unified autoregressive Transformer, unlike conventional ViT-based MLLMs that rely on external vision encoders. By tokenizing pixel patches directly, native MLLMs can suffer from severe visual token redundancy, which increases computational cost. Visual token compression aims to prune or merge redundant tokens to accelerate inference, but prior methods were designed for ViT-based architectures.

## [SOPO: Socratic-Guided RL for Span-Level Hallucination Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32212) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SOPO introduces a multi-turn reinforcement learning framework with a Socratic teacher-guided feedback mechanism to improve span-level hallucination detection in LLMs. The 4B model achieves state-of-the-art performance on RAGTruth, surpassing GPT-5 and standard RL baselines, with code and models released. This work extends reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) from structured reasoning tasks to the more ambiguous problem of span-level hallucination detection, potentially enabling more reliable LLM outputs in retrieval-augmented generation. The release of models and code facilitates further research and practical adoption. SOPO uses a teacher model to provide indirect hints inferred from ground truth during rollout, alleviating reward sparsity on hard examples, and incorporates Turn-based Advantage Scaling to penalize verbosity bias. The 4B model outperforms GRPO on four out-of-domain benchmarks but shows mixed evidence against CAPO.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 3, 05:00

**Background**: Large language models often generate hallucinations—content that is unfaithful or inconsistent with facts. Span-level hallucination detection aims to pinpoint the exact text spans that are hallucinated, which is more challenging than sequence-level detection. Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) has been successful in tasks with structured answers like math and coding, but applying it to hallucination detection faces challenges such as localization ambiguity and reward sparsity.

## [LLM-SC adapted to sacred heritage with new taxonomy and human evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35685) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers adapted the LLM-Social Computing (LLM-SC) framework to Pashupatinath Temple reviews by defining a 12-type entity taxonomy (four original plus eight sacred-heritage types), four relation types, and nine aspect-sentiment dimensions in a GPT-4o extraction prompt. Applied to 2,500 English Tripadvisor reviews, the pipeline produced 11,286 entity, 6,232 aspect-sentiment, and 5,590 relation records, with the eight new sacred-heritage types accounting for 64.8% of entity records. This work shows how generic LLM extraction schemes can miss culturally specific elements like rituals, sacred spaces, and emotional nuances at living heritage sites, and it offers a reusable domain-specific schema for cultural heritage NLP. It also provides a component-wise human evaluation benchmark that highlights the gap between LLM outputs and expert annotation in niche domains. Against an adjudicated two-annotator reference for 200 reviews, exact normalized surface-and-type matching yielded provisional entity scores of 41.0% precision, 21.8% recall, and 28.4% micro-F1; aspect-presence F1 was 53.6% and end-to-end aspect-plus-polarity F1 was 42.6%. Human adjudication supported 153 of 194 first-listed relation candidates (78.9%), but this candidate-level rate does not estimate relation recall or whole-graph precision, and the authors explicitly note reproducibility and generalization limitations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 3, 05:00

**Background**: LLM-Social Computing (LLM-SC) is a framework that uses large language models to extract structured social-science data from unstructured text such as reviews. Information extraction with LLMs often relies on entity taxonomies and prompts to identify entities, relations, and sentiments, but generic taxonomies may not capture domain-specific concepts. Pashupatinath Temple is a living sacred heritage site in Nepal where visitor reviews reflect rituals, cultural restrictions, and mixed emotions that standard schemes may overlook.

## [DeFT-RAG Decomposes Multi-Hop Questions into Self-Contained Sub-Questions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37826) ⭐️ 6.0/10

DeFT-RAG, a new retrieval-augmented generation framework, generates one self-contained sub-question at a time from the original question and previously answered QA pairs, eliminating the need for planning, dependency tracking, and rewriting. It also offloads rule-based QA table updates and yes/no decisions to a fine-tuned lightweight model, and caches QA pairs for reuse, achieving the best accuracy among thirteen baselines on MuSiQue, 2WikiMultiHopQA, and HotpotQA with gpt-4o-mini and Qwen3.6 backbones. Multi-hop questions remain a key weakness for RAG systems, and DeFT-RAG's approach reduces LLM workload and token usage while improving evidence-chain recovery, potentially making RAG more reliable for complex queries. The framework's efficiency gains, such as 12.9% fewer LLM calls with a shared QA cache, could lower costs and latency in real-world applications. On a shared QA cache across 1,000 MuSiQue questions, 23.1% of sub-questions were answered from the cache, reducing LLM calls by 12.9% without accuracy loss. The approach uses a fine-tuned lightweight decision model for yes/no decisions and rule-based updates to a QA table, and it gives the LLM fewer input tokens than any other iterative method.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 3, 05:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) enhances large language models by retrieving relevant passages from a corpus to ground answers. Multi-hop questions require chaining facts across documents via intermediate entities not mentioned in the question, which standard RAG struggles with. Graph-based RAG retrieves once from named entities and often misses the full evidence chain, while iterative RAG recovers it step by step but burdens the LLM with multiple tasks like planning and rewriting.

## [Xiaohongshu users link audience change to algorithms and platform growth, study finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38026) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.38026v2) analyzes 262 comments from eight high-engagement Xiaohongshu threads about a perceived World Cup-related audience influx, using thematic analysis and permutation-based association analysis. Among 70 comments with explicit attributions and strategies, the study found a strong association (Cramér's V = .583) between how users explained the change and how they managed community boundaries. The findings connect users' causal sensemaking about audience shifts to concrete governance outcomes on recommendation-driven platforms, suggesting that how people explain who is arriving shapes whether they respond with technical fixes, social enforcement, or normative socialization. This matters for platform designers and policymakers interested in community health and boundary negotiation. The study is qualitative and based on a small sample of 262 comments, with the key association analysis resting on only 70 comments that contained both explicit attributions and strategies. Algorithmic attributions concentrated in technical boundary management, while social-actor attributions aligned more with social enforcement or normative socialization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 3, 05:00

**Background**: Xiaohongshu (Little Red Book) is a Chinese social media and e-commerce platform founded in 2013 that combines user-generated lifestyle content with product discovery and shopping. Recommendation-driven platforms can reshape not only what users see but also who becomes visible, making audience change a site of community boundary negotiation. Cramér's V is a statistical measure of association between two categorical variables, ranging from 0 to 1, and permutation-based association analysis assesses the strength of such relationships by comparing observed values against randomly permuted data.

## [NECA: Neighbor Event Curve Augmentation for ICU Deterioration Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40420) ⭐️ 6.0/10

NECA is a model-agnostic method that retrieves similar historical ICU patients and converts their organ dysfunction event onset times into cumulative event-curve features over 6h, 12h, 24h, and 42h horizons. On PhysioNet 2012 and a 4,000-stay MIMIC-IV subset, it improves average PR-AUC and ROC-AUC across four organ dysfunction tasks and seven supervised learners, with the largest gain for neurological events (PR-AUC 0.461 to 0.557 on PhysioNet 2012). This work shows that leveraging the event timing of similar historical patients can provide a structured temporal risk signal, potentially improving early warning systems for ICU organ dysfunction without requiring changes to existing predictive models. It is relevant to healthcare ML researchers and clinicians interested in retrieval-based augmentation for time-series clinical data. The method is evaluated on two modest datasets (PhysioNet 2012 and a 4,000-stay MIMIC-IV subset), and ablations indicate that clinically meaningful retrieval and horizon-specific neighbor risk drive most of the improvement, while the full cumulative curve adds task-dependent temporal context. The source code will be released upon paper acceptance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 3, 05:00

**Background**: ICU patients generate irregular and heterogeneous clinical measurements, making short-term organ-specific risk prediction challenging. Retrieval augmentation is a technique that equips models with external information by fetching relevant examples, commonly used in large language models but increasingly applied to other domains. NECA adapts this idea to clinical time series by retrieving similar patients and using their event timing as additional features for standard supervised predictors.
